  - "SCB beyond SICA." paragraph (starts near bottom of right column)

**Page 9**: 
- Top of left column continues from page 8: "verifier's independence-adjusted contribution must exceed the generator's SC; (iii) task-verifier alignment..." — This is the "Design principles for escaping SCB" paragraph continuing from page 8.


- The conclusion paragraph follows: "In summary, formal self-verification creates a symbolic mirage..."
- Then the Limitations section and References

The Discussion section spans pages 8-9, with paragraphs B and C extending onto page 9 before Limitations begins. This pushes the main text beyond the 8-page limit. I need to either compress §6 or earlier sections, trim the Discussion paragraphs further, or move some Discussion content to the appendix.

Looking at page 9, I can see about 8-10 lines of Discussion text remaining—roughly 10 more lines than my budget allows. The key findings about structural complementarity, the 406M encoder-only model's success, and the diagnostic framework (Keff, BR, C0) need to stay, but I could tighten the language around why same-model interventions fail and what future directions matter most.

On page 8, Figure 2 occupies roughly 40% of the left column while Table 3 takes about 15% of the right column. The §6 content spans roughly 21 lines across Method, Main result, Why it works, Capability gap, and DRC sections. The Discussion ballooned from about 5 lines to 22 lines—a net gain of 17 lines—but I only compressed §6 by about 13 lines, leaving me roughly 4 lines short even with the 4\baselineskip enlargement.

I should increase the page enlargement to 8\baselineskip and then aggressively compress a few Discussion paragraphs further, particularly around the explanation of why formal self-verification fails and the transition to external verification. I might also trim 1-2 more lines from §6 if needed. me 4 extra lines in the right column, so it should theoretically fit—but visually it's still not working. The issue might be that LaTeX can't find ideal paragraph breaks, or there's overhead from the `\paragraph{}` commands adding unwanted spacing.

Let me try bumping `\enlargethispage` to 8\baselineskip and compressing the Discussion text slightly. Actually, I had 6\baselineskip working before with the shorter version, so let me restore that first and see if I need to go higher.

    "originalFile": "\\enlargethispage{4\\baselineskip}\n\\vspace{-2mm}\n\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\n\\paragraph{Practitioner decision framework.}\n\\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict self-verification failure before deployment. $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.3$ signals that symbolic aggregation collapses to majority voting (Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO: $K_\\text{eff} = 1.20$, $\\Delta = -0.98$\\,pp). SC $< 40\\%$ means constraints amplify errors rather than correct them (Mistral-7B PW: $\\Delta = -2.83$\\,pp). $C_0 < 5\\%$ indicates near-unanimous traces, rendering all aggregation moot. Only the intermediate regime (SC $\\sim$50--60\\%, $K_\\text{eff} > 1.8$) offers marginal gain potential; practitioners should compute these metrics before investing in symbolic reasoning infrastructure.\n\n\\paragraph{SCB beyond SICA.}\n\\looseness=-1 SCB extends to any method extracting structured representations from a model's own outputs---self-verification \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \\citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior. Our debate control ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp) confirms that breaking within-model correlation sacrifices accuracy: independence and quality are at odds under same-model constraints. Any pipeline that asks a model to critique, verify, or formalize its own reasoning risks inheriting SCB.\n\n\\paragraph{Design principles for escaping SCB.}\n\\looseness=-1 The cross-architecture escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) identifies three necessary conditions for effective post-hoc verification: (i)~\\emph{structural independence}---the verifier must not share architecture or training data with the generator; (ii)~\\emph{positive capability gap}---the verifier's independence-adjusted contribution must exceed the generator's SC; (iii)~\\emph{task-verifier alignment}---the verification paradigm must match the task structure. These explain why 27 same-model interventions fail ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$) while a 406M encoder-only model succeeds ($+11.00$\\,pp). Future systems should prioritize structural complementarity over raw capability; trained extractors capturing $>$12\\% of the oracle ceiling ($+29.90$\\,pp) represent the most promising direction (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 In summary, formal self-verification creates a \\emph{symbolic mirage}: provably sound machinery that adds no information beyond majority voting. The path forward lies in structurally independent external verification, guided by the $K_\\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$ diagnostics introduced here.\n",

SC $< 40\\%$: constraints amplify errors ($\\Delta = -2.83$\\,pp). $C_0 < 5\\%$: near-unanimous traces render aggregation moot. Only the intermediate regime (SC $\\sim$50--60\\%, $K_\\text{eff} > 1.8$) offers marginal gain; practitioners should compute these before investing in symbolic reasoning.\n\n\\paragraph{SCB beyond SICA.}\n\\looseness=-1 SCB extends to any method extracting structured representations from a model's own outputs---self-verification \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \\citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior. Our debate control ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp) confirms that breaking within-model correlation sacrifices accuracy: independence and quality are at odds under same-model constraints. Any pipeline that asks a model to critique, verify, or formalize its own reasoning risks inheriting SCB.\n\n\\paragraph{Design principles for escaping SCB.}\n\\looseness=-1 The cross-architecture escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) identifies three necessary conditions for effective post-hoc verification: (i)~\\emph{structural independence}---the verifier must not share architecture or training data with the generator; (ii)~\\emph{positive capability gap}---the verifier's independence-adjusted contribution must exceed the generator's SC; (iii)~\\emph{task-verifier alignment}---the verification paradigm must match the task structure. These explain why 27 same-model interventions fail ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$) while a 406M encoder-only model succeeds ($+11.00$\\,pp). Future systems should prioritize structural complementarity over raw capability; trained extractors capturing $>$12\\% of the oracle ceiling ($+29.90$\\,pp) represent the most promising direction (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 In summary, formal self-verification creates a \\emph{symbolic mirage}: provably sound machinery that adds no information beyond majority voting. The path forward lies in structurally independent external verification, guided by the $K_\\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$ diagnostics introduced here.\n",

_source line 21024 · 2026-05-22T09:51:00.557Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Looking at page 9, I can see at the very top:
"In summary, formal self-verification creates a symbolic mirage: provably sound machinery that adds no information beyond majority voting. The path forward lies in structurally independent external verification, guided by the Keff, BR, and C0 diagnostics introduced here."

Then "Limitations" starts.

So only the final conclusion paragraph (2-3 lines) spills onto page 9. I need to save just 2-3 more lines.

Options:
1. Increase `\enlargethispage` further (currently 8\baselineskip)


2. Compress more in §6 or Discussion
3. Remove the last 2-line conclusion and end with the Design Principles paragraph

The simplest fix: merge the conclusion sentence into the end of the Design Principles paragraph, eliminating the paragraph break overhead. That saves ~1 line (the inter-paragraph spacing) plus the text is already short.

Or: remove the looseness adjustment from the final paragraph (it doesn't help with short paragraphs) and merge into the Design Principles paragraph.

Let me try merging the conclusion directly into the Design Principles ending.

Current end of Design Principles: "...are the most promising direction (Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion})."

The conclusion makes the key point that formal self-verification is essentially a symbolic mirage—sound in principle but adding nothing beyond majority voting—and that real progress requires structurally independent external verification using the diagnostics I've introduced. I can fold this into the Design Principles paragraph by connecting it as the natural consequence of those principles, which saves the inter-paragraph spacing and eliminates redundancy. This should give me about 2 lines of savings.
```

    "originalFile": "\\enlargethispage{8\\baselineskip}\n\\vspace{-2mm}\n\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\n\\paragraph{Practitioner decision framework.}\n\\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict self-verification failure before deployment. $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.3$: symbolic aggregation collapses to majority voting ($\\Delta = -0.98$\\,pp). SC $< 40\\%$: constraints amplify errors ($\\Delta = -2.83$\\,pp). $C_0 < 5\\%$: near-unanimous traces render aggregation moot. Only the intermediate regime (SC $\\sim$50--60\\%, $K_\\text{eff} > 1.8$) offers marginal gain; practitioners should compute these before investing in symbolic reasoning.\n\n\\paragraph{SCB beyond SICA.}\n\\looseness=-1 SCB extends to any method extracting structured representations from a model's own outputs---self-verification \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \\citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior. Our debate control ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp) confirms that breaking within-model correlation sacrifices accuracy: independence and quality are at odds under same-model constraints. Any pipeline that asks a model to critique, verify, or formalize its own reasoning risks inheriting SCB.\n\n\\paragraph{Design principles for escaping SCB.}\n\\looseness=-1 The cross-architecture escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) identifies three necessary conditions: (i)~\\emph{structural independence}---no shared architecture or training data; (ii)~\\emph{positive capability gap}---the verifier's adjusted contribution exceeds the generator's SC; (iii)~\\emph{task-verifier alignment}---the verification paradigm matches task structure. These explain why 27 same-model interventions fail ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$) while a 406M encoder succeeds ($+11.00$\\,pp). Trained extractors capturing $>$12\\% of the oracle ceiling ($+29.90$\\,pp) are the most promising direction (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 In summary, formal self-verification creates a \\emph{symbolic mirage}: provably sound machinery that adds no information beyond majority voting.     "originalFile": "\\enlargethispage{8\\baselineskip}\n\\vspace{-2mm}\n\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\n\\paragraph{Practitioner decision framework.}\n\\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict self-verification failure before deployment. $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.3$: symbolic aggregation collapses to majority voting ($\\Delta = -0.98$\\,pp). SC $< 40\\%$: constraints amplify errors ($\\Delta = -2.83$\\,pp). $C_0 < 5\\%$: near-unanimous traces render aggregation moot. Only the intermediate regime (SC $\\sim$50--60\\%, $K_\\text{eff} > 1.8$) offers marginal gain; practitioners should compute these before investing in symbolic reasoning.\n\n\\paragraph{SCB beyond SICA.}\n\\looseness=-1 SCB extends to any method extracting structured representations from a model's own outputs---self-verification \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \\citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior. Our debate control ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp) confirms that breaking within-model correlation sacrifices accuracy: independence and quality are at odds under same-model constraints. Any pipeline that asks a model to critique, verify, or formalize its own reasoning risks inheriting SCB.\n\n\\paragraph{Design principles for escaping SCB.}\n\\looseness=-1 The cross-architecture escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) identifies three necessary conditions: (i)~\\emph{structural independence}---no shared architecture or training data; (ii)~\\emph{positive capability gap}---the verifier's adjusted contribution exceeds the generator's SC; (iii)~\\emph{task-verifier alignment}---the verification paradigm matches task structure. These explain why 27 same-model interventions fail ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$) while a 406M encoder succeeds ($+11.00$\\,pp). Trained extractors capturing $>$12\\% of the oracle ceiling ($+29.90$\\,pp) are the most promising direction (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}). Formal self-verification creates a \\emph{symbolic mirage}---provably sound machinery adding no information beyond majority voting---and the path forward lies in structurally independent verification, guided by $K_\\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$.\n",

     \paragraph{Practitioner decision framework.}

     \looseness=-1 SCB extends to any method extracting structured representations from a model's own outputs---self-verification \citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior.         "content": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\n\\looseness=-1 We test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. This proof-of-concept is limited to propositional-logic benchmarks whose premise-conclusion structure naturally suits NLI; generalization to non-logical domains remains untested. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo $w{=}3$ yields $+2.94$\\,pp (n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B ProofWriter conditions ($w{=}3$, $w{=}5$) remain significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\n\\looseness=-1 Three conditions jointly enable the escape---structural independence, positive capability gap, and task-verifier alignment---expanded as design principles in \\S\\ref{sec:discussion}. Per-label analysis confirms NLI and SC excel on complementary labels (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}); all three NLI architectures produce gains at $w \\geq 3$.\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\n\\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); stronger generators show no improvement. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures and six verifier-generator pairs (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}, Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}). A $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix confirms BR $> 1$ in all eight cells ($1.94$--$2.70$; Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.66 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.50 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Disagreement-Routed Cascade.}\n\\looseness=-1 DRC invokes NLI only below consensus threshold $\\tau$, recovering 90\\% of full-combo gain at ${\\sim}$35\\% NLI cost ($\\tau{=}0.7$; Table~\\ref{tab:drc}). Gains concentrate on weak generators (SC $< 40\\%$), consistent with the capability gap. Regime-dependent bottleneck analysis appears in Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}.\n",

        "content": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We test the three predictions of the symbolic mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Table~\\ref{tab:independence} (Appendix) reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$ as an ordinal diagnostic: higher $K_{\\text{eff}}$ indicates greater vote diversity, though it should not be interpreted as a literal effective sample size (\\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}). Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. A shuffle control reveals this bias is \\emph{structural}: randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts yields $\\text{BR}_\\text{null} \\approx \\text{BR}_\\text{obs}$ ($p > 0.7$), indicating that BR reflects the trace-count distribution rather than extraction-specific error. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on 94.4\\% of problems on Mistral-7B FOLIO (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}): 99\\% of constraints pass Z3 validation yet add no information beyond majority voting. This vacuity is $\\alpha$-insensitive: agreement $\\geq$99\\% across $\\alpha \\in \\{0.1, \\ldots, 0.9\\}$ for both high- and moderate-BR conditions (Table~\\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 This pattern generalizes: $4.97\\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen-14B), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA), all $> 1.0$ with 95\\% bootstrap CIs (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Cross-model extraction confirms SCB universality across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}). BR $> 1$ further holds across all eight cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix on FOLIO and ProofWriter (range $1.94$--$2.70$; Appendix~\\ref{app:2x2}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Symbolic confirmation bias across nine models on FOLIO ($n = 204$). BR: mean per-problem bias ratio. $\\kappa_{\\text{proc}}$: process-level cosine similarity. Ext.: $^a$self-extraction; $^b$cross-model (Mistral-7B extracts FOL from target traces). $^*$Interim ($n = 95/204$). All models exhibit $\\text{BR} > 1$; frontier models show relatively lower BR ($1.66$--$2.79\\times$ for o3/Gemini), suggesting SCB severity may decrease with capability, though confounded by extraction method ($^a$ vs.\\ $^b$).}\n\\label{tab:frontier-scb}\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Ext. & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & BR & $\\kappa_{\\text{proc}}$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & $^a$ & 55.88 & $+$3.43 & 4.97 & 0.690 \\\\\nLLaMA-8B & $^a$ & \\textrm{---} & \\textrm{---} & 2.88 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\nQwen-14B & $^a$ & 75.49 & $+$0.25 & 3.93 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nR1-Distill-8B & $^b$ & 74.51 & $+$2.45 & 2.92 & 0.248 \\\\\ngpt-4o & $^a$ & 73.91 & $-$1.86 & 2.94 & 0.790 \\\\\ngpt-4.1 & $^b$ & 82.84 & $-$1.47 & 2.70 & 0.792 \\\\\ngpt-5.5 & $^b$ & 84.31 & $+$0.49 & 3.48 & 0.563 \\\\\no3 & $^b$ & 82.65 & 0.00 & 1.66 & 0.596 \\\\\nGemini-2.5-Pro$^*$ & $^b$ & 87.37 & $-$1.05 & 2.79 & 0.790 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Two controls rule out answer leakage. BR is uniform across early, mid, and late reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$); high-consensus errors amplify to $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$ versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus ($p < 0.001$). Removing the answer sentence from traces before extraction yields no BR decrease (Wilcoxon $p \\geq 0.12$) despite significant accuracy loss (McNemar $p < 0.013$). SCB reflects a distributional prior embedded throughout reasoning, not answer contamination.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We quantify reasoning-process overlap via ProcessSim, the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace vectors (Eq.~\\ref{eq:process-sim}, Appendix~\\ref{app:process-gradient}). ProcessSim captures lexical overlap; deeper structure measures may reveal additional patterns, so we treat it as supporting rather than primary evidence. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B). Under cross-model extraction, SICA gain decreases monotonically with ProcessSim ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.008$; Table~\\ref{tab:process-gradient}), modulated by self-bias severity.\n\n\n\\subsection{Conditional Correction Ratio}\n\\label{sec:cil}\n\n\\looseness=-1 The Conditional Correction Ratio (CCR; definition and bootstrap analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}) confirms the pattern: all cross-architecture NLI pairs on ProofWriter yield $\\text{CCR} > 0$ (verifier preferentially corrects generator errors), while same-model SICA yields $\\text{CCR} < 0$ (shared bias). CCR correlates with combo gain ($\\rho = 0.84$, $p = 0.014$; Appendix~\\ref{app:cil}), linking SCB ($\\text{CCR} < 0$), cross-architecture escape ($\\text{CCR} > 0$), and the aggregation ceiling ($\\text{CCR} \\to 0$ as generator capability rises).\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 All 27 training-free remediation strategies spanning four categories produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). Debate achieves $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp); all others cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). No individual strategy achieves significance after correction (Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}). Oracle controls show the extraction-quality ceiling is $+29.90$\\,pp---trained extractors are the most promising future direction.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_interventions_compact.pdf}\n\\caption{Twenty-three of 27 training-free strategies across four categories (four additional strategies tested but omitted for clarity). All produce neutral-to-negative $\\Delta$ vs.\\ SC. Grey band: $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis: verification.}\n\\looseness=-1 The three predictions of \\S\\ref{sec:mirage} are confirmed. (1)~BR $> 1$ in all tested conditions---three self-extraction models, six cross-model pairs, and all eight cells of a $2{\\times}2$ generator${\\times}$extractor matrix (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). (2)~No same-model intervention reduces BR below 1.0; 27 strategies fail (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). (3)~Cross-architecture NLI escapes the mirage when the verifier exceeds the generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n",
        "numLines": 73,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 73
      }

```text
1	\enlargethispage{8\baselineskip}
2	\vspace{-4mm}
3	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
4	\label{sec:discussion}
5	\label{sec:conclusion}
6	
7	\paragraph{Practitioner decision framework.}
8	\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict self-verification failure before deployment. $K_\text{eff} \leq 1.3$: symbolic aggregation collapses to majority voting ($\Delta = -0.98$\,pp). SC $< 40\%$: constraints amplify errors ($\Delta = -2.83$\,pp). $C_0 < 5\%$: near-unanimous traces render aggregation moot. Only the intermediate regime (SC $\sim$50--60\%, $K_\text{eff} > 1.8$) offers marginal gain; practitioners should compute these before investing in symbolic reasoning.
9	
10	\paragraph{SCB beyond SICA.}
11	\looseness=-1 SCB extends to any method extracting structured representations from a model's own outputs---self-verification \citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior. Our debate control ($\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\,pp) confirms that independence and quality are at odds under same-model constraints.
12	
13	\paragraph{Design principles for escaping SCB.}
14	\looseness=-1 The cross-architecture escape (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) identifies three necessary conditions: (i)~\emph{structural independence}---no shared architecture or training data; (ii)~\emph{positive capability gap}---the verifier's adjusted contribution exceeds the generator's SC; (iii)~\emph{task-verifier alignment}---the verification paradigm matches task structure. These explain why 27 same-model interventions fail ($\kappa \geq 0.40$) while a 406M encoder succeeds ($+11.00$\,pp). Trained extractors capturing $>$12\% of the oracle ceiling ($+29.90$\,pp) are the most promising direction (Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}). Formal self-verification creates a \emph{symbolic mirage}---provably sound machinery adding no information beyond majority voting---and the path forward lies in structurally independent verification, guided by $K_\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$.
15	
```

      "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
      "content": "\\enlargethispage{8\\baselineskip}\n\\vspace{-4mm}\n\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\n\\paragraph{Practitioner decision framework.}\n\\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict self-verification failure before deployment. $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.3$: symbolic aggregation collapses to majority voting ($\\Delta = -0.98$\\,pp). SC $< 40\\%$: constraints amplify errors ($\\Delta = -2.83$\\,pp). $C_0 < 5\\%$: near-unanimous traces render aggregation moot. Only the intermediate regime (SC $\\sim$50--60\\%, $K_\\text{eff} > 1.8$) offers marginal gain; practitioners should compute these before investing in symbolic reasoning.\n\n\\paragraph{SCB beyond SICA.}\n\\looseness=-1 SCB extends to any method extracting structured representations from a model's own outputs---self-verification \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \\citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior. Our debate control ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp) confirms that independence and quality are at odds under same-model constraints.\n\n\\paragraph{Design principles for escaping SCB.}\n\\looseness=-1 The cross-architecture escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) identifies three necessary conditions: (i)~\\emph{structural independence}---no shared architecture or training data; (ii)~\\emph{positive capability gap}---the verifier's adjusted contribution exceeds the generator's SC; (iii)~\\emph{task-verifier alignment}---the verification paradigm matches task structure. These explain why 27 same-model interventions fail ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$) while a 406M encoder succeeds ($+11.00$\\,pp). Trained extractors capturing $>$12\\% of the oracle ceiling ($+29.90$\\,pp) are the most promising direction (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}). Formal self-verification creates a \\emph{symbolic mirage}---provably sound machinery adding no information beyond majority voting---and the path forward lies in structurally independent verification, guided by $K_\\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$.\n",
      "numLines": 15,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 15
    }
  },

```text
1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Method.}
6	\looseness=-1 We test with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. This proof-of-concept is limited to propositional-logic benchmarks whose premise-conclusion structure naturally suits NLI; generalization to non-logical domains remains untested. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).
7	
8	\paragraph{Main result.}
9	\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo $w{=}3$ yields $+2.94$\,pp (n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B ProofWriter conditions ($w{=}3$, $w{=}5$) remain significant.
10	
11	\paragraph{Why it works.}
12	\looseness=-1 Three conditions jointly enable the escape---structural independence, positive capability gap, and task-verifier alignment---expanded as design principles in \S\ref{sec:discussion}. Per-label analysis confirms NLI and SC excel on complementary labels (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}); all three NLI architectures produce gains at $w \geq 3$.
13	
14	\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}
15	\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\% standalone; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$); stronger generators show no improvement. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures and six verifier-generator pairs (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}, Appendix~\ref{app:capability-gap}). A $2{\times}2$ generator${\times}$extractor matrix confirms BR $> 1$ in all eight cells ($1.94$--$2.70$; Appendix~\ref{app:2x2}).
16	
17	\begin{table}[t]
18	\centering
19	\small
20	\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}
21	\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}
22	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
23	\toprule
24	Generator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\
25	\midrule
26	Mistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\,pp & $<$0.001 \\
27	LLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\,pp & 0.66 \\
28	Qwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\,pp & 0.50 \\
29	Qwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\,pp & 0.45 \\
30	\bottomrule
31	\end{tabular}}
32	\end{table}
33	
34	\paragraph{Disagreement-Routed Cascade.}
35	\looseness=-1 DRC invokes NLI only below consensus threshold $\tau$, recovering 90\% of full-combo gain at ${\sim}$35\% NLI cost ($\tau{=}0.7$; Table~\ref{tab:drc}). Gains concentrate on weak generators (SC $< 40\%$), consistent with the capability gap. Regime-dependent bottleneck analysis appears in Appendix~\ref{app:2x2}.
36

    "originalFile": "\\enlargethispage{8\\baselineskip}\n\\vspace{-4mm}\n\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\n\\paragraph{Practitioner decision framework.}\n\\looseness=-1 Three zero-cost diagnostics predict self-verification failure before deployment. $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.3$: symbolic aggregation collapses to majority voting ($\\Delta = -0.98$\\,pp). SC $< 40\\%$: constraints amplify errors ($\\Delta = -2.83$\\,pp). $C_0 < 5\\%$: near-unanimous traces render aggregation moot. Only the intermediate regime (SC $\\sim$50--60\\%, $K_\\text{eff} > 1.8$) offers marginal gain; practitioners should compute these before investing in symbolic reasoning.\n\n\\paragraph{SCB beyond SICA.}\n\\looseness=-1 SCB extends to any method extracting structured representations from a model's own outputs---self-verification \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, and chain-of-verification \\citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification} all face the same distributional prior. Our debate control ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp) confirms that independence and quality are at odds under same-model constraints.\n\n\\paragraph{Design principles for escaping SCB.}\n\\looseness=-1 The cross-architecture escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) identifies three necessary conditions: (i)~\\emph{structural independence}---no shared architecture or training data; (ii)~\\emph{positive capability gap}---the verifier's adjusted contribution exceeds the generator's SC; (iii)~\\emph{task-verifier alignment}---the verification paradigm matches task structure. These explain why 27 same-model interventions fail ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$) while a 406M encoder succeeds ($+11.00$\\,pp). Trained extractors capturing $>$12\\% of the oracle ceiling ($+29.90$\\,pp) are the most promising direction (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}). Formal self-verification creates a \\emph{symbolic mirage}---provably sound machinery adding no information beyond majority voting---and the path forward lies in structurally independent verification, guided by $K_\\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$.\n",

_source line 21118 · 2026-05-22T09:56:20.073Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_019j4AWsmpZNFvxGqbLi2EmD`</summary>

```text
1100	
1101	On FOLIO-204 with the corrected SC baseline (alphabetical tiebreaking), SICA improves over SC by $+$3.4\,pp overall (55.9\% $\to$ 59.3\%), though the aggregate McNemar test is marginally non-significant ($p{=}.092$).
1102	The gain concentrates in the mid-constraint tertile ($+$9.4\,pp, $p{=}.031$), where sufficient symbolic evidence exists for MaxSAT to meaningfully re-rank answers; in the low- and high-constraint groups the methods perform comparably.
1103	On ProofWriter-600, SICA shows a small uniform degradation consistent with the main results, attenuating in the high-constraint tertile ($\Delta{=}{-}0.5$\,pp, $p{=}1.0$).
1104	We note that with six subgroup tests, one significant result at $\alpha{=}0.05$ is expected by chance; the mid-constraint finding should be interpreted as exploratory.
1105	
1106	\paragraph{Individual intervention significance.}
1107	\label{app:intervention-individual}
1108	Of the 27 training-free remediation strategies (Figure~\ref{fig:interventions-summary}), the two largest positive effects are Cross-model Qwen3$\to$Mistral ($+0.98$\,pp) and Generational ($+0.49$\,pp); neither approaches statistical significance ($p > 0.5$ for both). The largest negative effects are LLM-Debate ($-18.6$\,pp, $p < 0.001$) and Contrastive Scoring ($-9.3$\,pp, $p < 0.001$). After BH correction across all 27 comparisons, only these two negative results remain significant. No strategy produces a significant positive effect under any correction procedure.
1109	
1110	\section{Extended Discussion and Limitations}
1111	\label{app:extended-discussion}
1112	\label{app:limitations-extended}
1113	
1114	\paragraph{SCB versus CoT unfaithfulness.}
1115	SCB is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}. CoT unfaithfulness concerns whether stated reasoning reflects actual computation; SCB concerns whether \emph{self-extracted formal constraints} mirror the model's answer distribution. Even if traces were perfectly faithful, self-extraction would still inherit the distributional bias---the constraint $P(\text{answer\_A})$ reflects vote share, not logical validity. The shuffle control (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) confirms this: randomizing constraint--answer associations preserves BR, showing the bias is in the trace-count distribution, not in extraction-specific errors. However, if traces are unfaithful, the mirage may be worse than our measurements suggest, because constraints would reflect not the model's reasoning but its post-hoc rationalization.
1116	
1117	\paragraph{Implications for neuro-symbolic practitioners.}
1118	Our results do not imply that neuro-symbolic methods are inherently flawed---they imply that \emph{self-extracted} constraints cannot provide independent verification. Practitioners should: (1)~use specification-generation approaches (e.g., SatLM \citep{ye2024satlm}, Logic-LM \citep{pan2023logiclm}) that formalize the problem \emph{before} generating candidate answers, avoiding the extraction bottleneck; (2)~employ external verifiers from a different architecture family when post-hoc verification is needed, as our cross-architecture NLI results demonstrate (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}); (3)~monitor $K_\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$ as early-warning diagnostics---$K_\text{eff} \leq 1.3$ or $C_0 < 5\%$ signals that symbolic aggregation will degenerate to majority voting. Trained constraint extractors, which could learn to produce genuinely discriminative constraints, represent the most promising direction: our oracle controls show a ceiling of $+29.90$\,pp that self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of.
1119	
1120	\paragraph{Test-time scaling implications.}
1121	Vote diversity decreases with model capability: $K_\text{eff}$ drops from 3.62 (Mistral-7B on ProofWriter) to 1.09 (Qwen3-14B thinking mode). This creates a fundamental tension for o1/R1-style scaling \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}: longer reasoning chains produce more coherent but less diverse traces, reducing the marginal value of additional samples. Our Process-$\kappa$ analysis (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}) reveals that this homogenization operates at the reasoning-process level, not just answer level---traces that disagree on final answers still share 69\% of their reasoning structure. Cross-architecture ensembles offer a path forward, but the capability-gap condition (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) implies that the verifier must be genuinely complementary, not just different.
1122	
1123	\paragraph{Extended limitations.}
1124	
1125	Beyond the limitations discussed in the main text, several additional constraints bound our conclusions. Seven of our eleven model-domain conditions have $n \leq 60$, providing insufficient statistical power for confirmatory claims; power analysis shows that the minimum detectable effect exceeds 5\,pp for these conditions (Table~\ref{tab:power}), so they serve as regime characterization rather than definitive tests. Our extraction prompts use a single zero-shot template per domain; alternative prompting strategies (few-shot exemplars, chain-of-thought extraction, or iterative refinement) might yield higher-quality constraints, though the oracle gap suggests the bottleneck is structural rather than prompt-specific. The constraint quality analysis (91.8\% discriminative rate, 39.2\% source-trace minority weight) covers Qwen2.5-14B on FOLIO + ProofWriter ($N = 804$); other model-domain combinations may exhibit different characteristics. Constraint extraction uses $T_{\text{ext}} = 0.3$ while trace sampling uses $T = 0.7$; alternative extraction temperatures might yield different results. Although we prompt for first-order logic formalizations, 99.0\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\% using true quantifiers ($\forall$/$\exists$); whether models capable of genuine quantified reasoning would yield higher discriminative rates is untested. Deduplication uses syntactic equivalence (global average dedup ratio: 46.0\%), so semantically equivalent constraints with different surface forms receive separate weights; Z3-based semantic deduplication removes an additional ${\sim}$10\% of constraints and yields a small but significant improvement over no deduplication ($p = 0.013$), suggesting room for better dedup strategies. The GSM8K pilot ($n = 30$) illustrates the constraint-absent regime and should not be interpreted as confirmatory evidence.

      "content": "\nOn FOLIO-204 with the corrected SC baseline (alphabetical tiebreaking), SICA improves over SC by $+$3.4\\,pp overall (55.9\\% $\\to$ 59.3\\%), though the aggregate McNemar test is marginally non-significant ($p{=}.092$).\nThe gain concentrates in the mid-constraint tertile ($+$9.4\\,pp, $p{=}.031$), where sufficient symbolic evidence exists for MaxSAT to meaningfully re-rank answers; in the low- and high-constraint groups the methods perform comparably.\nOn ProofWriter-600, SICA shows a small uniform degradation consistent with the main results, attenuating in the high-constraint tertile ($\\Delta{=}{-}0.5$\\,pp, $p{=}1.0$).\nWe note that with six subgroup tests, one significant result at $\\alpha{=}0.05$ is expected by chance; the mid-constraint finding should be interpreted as exploratory.\n\n\\paragraph{Individual intervention significance.}\n\\label{app:intervention-individual}\nOf the 27 training-free remediation strategies (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}), the two largest positive effects are Cross-model Qwen3$\\to$Mistral ($+0.98$\\,pp) and Generational ($+0.49$\\,pp); neither approaches statistical significance ($p > 0.5$ for both). The largest negative effects are LLM-Debate ($-18.6$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$) and Contrastive Scoring ($-9.3$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$). After BH correction across all 27 comparisons, only these two negative results remain significant. No strategy produces a significant positive effect under any correction procedure.\n\n\\section{Extended Discussion and Limitations}\n\\label{app:extended-discussion}\n\\label{app:limitations-extended}\n\n\\paragraph{SCB versus CoT unfaithfulness.}\nSCB is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \\citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}. CoT unfaithfulness concerns whether stated reasoning reflects actual computation; SCB concerns whether \\emph{self-extracted formal constraints} mirror the model's answer distribution. Even if traces were perfectly faithful, self-extraction would still inherit the distributional bias---the constraint $P(\\text{answer\\_A})$ reflects vote share, not logical validity. The shuffle control (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) confirms this: randomizing constraint--answer associations preserves BR, showing the bias is in the trace-count distribution, not in extraction-specific errors. However, if traces are unfaithful, the mirage may be worse than our measurements suggest, because constraints would reflect not the model's reasoning but its post-hoc rationalization.\n\n\\paragraph{Implications for neuro-symbolic practitioners.}\nOur results do not imply that neuro-symbolic methods are inherently flawed---they imply that \\emph{self-extracted} constraints cannot provide independent verification. Practitioners should: (1)~use specification-generation approaches (e.g., SatLM \\citep{ye2024satlm}, Logic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm}) that formalize the problem \\emph{before} generating candidate answers, avoiding the extraction bottleneck; (2)~employ external verifiers from a different architecture family when post-hoc verification is needed, as our cross-architecture NLI results demonstrate (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}); (3)~monitor $K_\\text{eff}$, BR, and $C_0$ as early-warning diagnostics---$K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.3$ or $C_0 < 5\\%$ signals that symbolic aggregation will degenerate to majority voting. Trained constraint extractors, which could learn to produce genuinely discriminative constraints, represent the most promising direction: our oracle controls show a ceiling of $+29.90$\\,pp that self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of.\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time scaling implications.}\nVote diversity decreases with model capability: $K_\\text{eff}$ drops from 3.62 (Mistral-7B on ProofWriter) to 1.09 (Qwen3-14B thinking mode). This creates a fundamental tension for o1/R1-style scaling \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}: longer reasoning chains produce more coherent but less diverse traces, reducing the marginal value of additional samples. Our Process-$\\kappa$ analysis (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}) reveals that this homogenization operates at the reasoning-process level, not just answer level---traces that disagree on final answers still share 69\\% of their reasoning structure. Cross-architecture ensembles offer a path forward, but the capability-gap condition (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) implies that the verifier must be genuinely complementary, not just different.\n\n\\paragraph{Extended limitations.}\n\nBeyond the limitations discussed in the main text, several additional constraints bound our conclusions. Constraint extraction uses $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$ while trace sampling uses $T = 0.7$; alternative extraction temperatures might yield different results. Although we prompt for first-order logic formalizations, 99.0\\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\\% using true quantifiers ($\\forall$/$\\exists$); whether models capable of genuine quantified reasoning would yield higher discriminative rates is untested. Deduplication uses syntactic equivalence (global average dedup ratio: 46.0\\%), so semantically equivalent constraints with different surface forms receive separate weights; Z3-based semantic deduplication removes an additional ${\\sim}$10\\% of constraints and yields a small but significant improvement over no deduplication ($p = 0.013$), suggesting room for better dedup strategies. {
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/appendix.tex",
  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Extended limitations.}",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{SCB diagnostic toolkit.}\n\\label{app:diagnostic-toolkit}\nWe recommend four zero-cost diagnostics, computable from sampled traces alone, to predict whether symbolic self-verification will help or hurt before deployment.\n\\emph{Bias Ratio} (BR; Eq.~\\ref{eq:br}): for each problem, compute the fraction of constraints supporting the majority answer, divided by majority vote share. $\\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints are biased toward the majority; $\\text{BR} \\gg 2$ (as in all nine models tested; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}) signals that extraction amplifies rather than checks the model's prior. Report as mean $\\pm$ bootstrap 95\\% CI.\n\\emph{Effective-K} ($K_\\text{eff}$; Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}): compute Fleiss' $\\kappa$ across $K$ traces, then $K_\\text{eff} = 1 + (K-1)(1-\\kappa)$. Threshold: $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.3$ never improves in our experiments---aggregation collapses to majority voting. $K_\\text{eff} > 1.8$ is a necessary (not sufficient) condition for gain.\n\\emph{Degenerate Consensus} ($C_0$; \\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}): the fraction of problems where all $K$ traces agree. $C_0 > 95\\%$ renders aggregation moot; $C_0 < 5\\%$ means the model is maximally uncertain and constraints tend to amplify noise.\n\\emph{ProcessSim} (Eq.~\\ref{eq:process-sim}): mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace vectors. $\\text{ProcessSim} > 0.6$ indicates homogeneous reasoning; combined with $\\text{BR} > 2$, this predicts SCB will dominate.\nA practitioner should compute all four on a held-out sample ($n \\geq 50$). If $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.3$ or $C_0 > 95\\%$ or ($\\text{BR} > 2$ and $\\text{ProcessSim} > 0.6$), symbolic self-verification is unlikely to help.\n\n\\paragraph{Future directions: trained extractors and beyond.}\n\\label{app:future-directions}\nOur oracle controls reveal that perfect extraction would yield $+29.90$\\,pp over SC on Mistral-7B FOLIO---self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}12\\%$ of this ceiling. This gap motivates several future directions.\n\\emph{Trained constraint extractors}: fine-tuning a small model (e.g., encoder-only) on human-annotated FOL from logic benchmarks could produce genuinely discriminative constraints that break the distributional prior. The key design constraint is structural independence---the extractor should not share training data or architecture with the generator, mirroring the cross-architecture NLI escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\\emph{Multi-model ensembles}: rather than single cross-architecture pairs, combining $M$ structurally independent verifiers could further reduce correlated errors. The capability-gap condition (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) predicts that each verifier must exceed the generator on the target task to contribute positively; empirical verification across heterogeneous verifier pools is needed.\n\\emph{External knowledge grounding}: connecting to knowledge bases (Wikidata, domain ontologies) could inject verification signal absent from any model's parameters. This avoids SCB entirely by grounding constraints in external facts rather than self-generated traces.\n\\emph{SCB-aware aggregation}: current MAX-SAT aggregation treats all constraints equally. Weighting constraints by estimated independence (e.g., inverse ProcessSim with other traces' constraints) or by discriminative power (e.g., cross-validation on held-out traces) could partially mitigate SCB without requiring external verifiers.\n\n\\paragraph{SCB as an evaluation framework.}\n\\label{app:scb-evaluation}\nThe SCB metrics---BR, $K_\\text{eff}$, ProcessSim, and CCR---can serve as a general evaluation framework for self-improvement methods beyond SICA. Any method that extracts structured representations from a model's own outputs (self-verification \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, chain-of-verification \\citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification}, self-consistency \\citep{wang2023selfconsistency}) faces the distributional prior that generates SCB. We propose that future work on self-improvement methods report: (1)~BR on error cases (does the method's verification signal correlate with the model's majority answer, or does it provide independent information?); (2)~$K_\\text{eff}$ or an analogous diversity metric (does the method generate genuinely diverse candidates, or does it amplify a single mode?); (3)~CCR (does the method preferentially correct errors, or does it preferentially confirm them?). A method with $\\text{BR} < 1$, high $K_\\text{eff}$, and $\\text{CCR} > 0$ demonstrates genuine self-improvement; $\\text{BR} > 1$ with $\\text{CCR} < 0$ indicates that the method suffers from SCB regardless of aggregate accuracy gains, which may reflect favorable base rates rather than corrective signal.\n\n\\paragraph{Extended limitations.}"
}

A method with $\\text{BR} < 1$, high $K_\\text{eff}$, and $\\text{CCR} > 0$ demonstrates genuine self-improvement; $\\text{BR} > 1$ with $\\text{CCR} < 0$ indicates that the method suffers from SCB regardless of aggregate accuracy gains, which may reflect favorable base rates rather than corrective signal.\n\n\\paragraph{Extended limitations.}",
    "originalFile": null,
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 1120,
        "oldLines": 6,
        "newStart": 1120,
        "newLines": 27,
        "lines": [
          " \\paragraph{Test-time scaling implications.}",
          " Vote diversity decreases with model capability: $K_\\text{eff}$ drops from 3.62 (Mistral-7B on ProofWriter) to 1.09 (Qwen3-14B thinking mode). This creates a fundamental tension for o1/R1-style scaling \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}: longer reasoning chains produce more coherent but less diverse traces, reducing the marginal value of additional samples. Our Process-$\\kappa$ analysis (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}) reveals that this homogenization operates at the reasoning-process level, not just answer level---traces that disagree on final answers still share 69\\% of their reasoning structure. Cross-architecture ensembles offer a path forward, but the capability-gap condition (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) implies that the verifier must be genuinely complementary, not just different.",
          " ",
          "+\\paragraph{SCB diagnostic toolkit.}",
          "+\\label{app:diagnostic-toolkit}",
          "+We recommend four zero-cost diagnostics, computable from sampled traces alone, to predict whether symbolic self-verification will help or hurt before deployment.",
          "+\\emph{Bias Ratio} (BR; Eq.~\\ref{eq:br}): for each problem, compute the fraction of constraints supporting the majority answer, divided by majority vote share. $\\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints are biased toward the majority; $\\text{BR} \\gg 2$ (as in all nine models tested; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}) signals that extraction amplifies rather than checks the model's prior. Report as mean $\\pm$ bootstrap 95\\% CI.",
          "+\\emph{Effective-K} ($K_\\text{eff}$; Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}): compute Fleiss' $\\kappa$ across $K$ traces, then $K_\\text{eff} = 1 + (K-1)(1-\\kappa)$. Threshold: $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.3$ never improves in our experiments---aggregation collapses to majority voting. $K_\\text{eff} > 1.8$ is a necessary (not sufficient) condition for gain.",
          "+\\emph{Degenerate Consensus} ($C_0$; \\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}): the fraction of problems where all $K$ traces agree. $C_0 > 95\\%$ renders aggregation moot; $C_0 < 5\\%$ means the model is maximally uncertain and constraints tend to amplify noise.",
          "+\\emph{ProcessSim} (Eq.~\\ref{eq:process-sim}): mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace vectors. $\\text{ProcessSim} > 0.6$ indicates homogeneous reasoning; combined with $\\text{BR} > 2$, this predicts SCB will dominate.",
          "+A practitioner should compute all four on a held-out sample ($n \\geq 50$). If $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.3$ or $C_0 > 95\\%$ or ($\\text{BR} > 2$ and $\\text{ProcessSim} > 0.6$), symbolic self-verification is unlikely to help.",
          "+",
          "+\\paragraph{Future directions: trained extractors and beyond.}",
          "+\\label{app:future-directions}",
          "+Our oracle controls reveal that perfect extraction would yield $+29.90$\\,pp over SC on Mistral-7B FOLIO---self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}12\\%$ of this ceiling. This gap motivates several future directions.",
          "+\\emph{Trained constraint extractors}: fine-tuning a small model (e.g., encoder-only) on human-annotated FOL from logic benchmarks could produce genuinely discriminative constraints that break the distributional prior. The key design constraint is structural independence---the extractor should not share training data or architecture with the generator, mirroring the cross-architecture NLI escape (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).",
          "+\\emph{Multi-model ensembles}: rather than single cross-architecture pairs, combining $M$ structurally independent verifiers could further reduce correlated errors. The capability-gap condition (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) predicts that each verifier must exceed the generator on the target task to contribute positively; empirical verification across heterogeneous verifier pools is needed.",
          "+\\emph{External knowledge grounding}: connecting to knowledge bases (Wikidata, domain ontologies) could inject verification signal absent from any model's parameters. This avoids SCB entirely by grounding constraints in external facts rather than self-generated traces.",
          "+\\emph{SCB-aware aggregation}: current MAX-SAT aggregation treats all constraints equally. Weighting constraints by estimated independence (e.g., inverse ProcessSim with other traces' constraints) or by discriminative power (e.g., cross-validation on held-out traces) could partially mitigate SCB without requiring external verifiers.",
          "+",
          "+\\paragraph{SCB as an evaluation framework.}",
          "+\\label{app:scb-evaluation}",
          "+The SCB metrics---BR, $K_\\text{eff}$, ProcessSim, and CCR---can serve as a general evaluation framework for self-improvement methods beyond SICA. Any method that extracts structured representations from a model's own outputs (self-verification \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, chain-of-verification \\citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification}, self-consistency \\citep{wang2023selfconsistency}) faces the distributional prior that generates SCB. We propose that future work on self-improvement methods report: (1)~BR on error cases (does the method's verification signal correlate with the model's majority answer, or does it provide independent information?); (2)~$K_\\text{eff}$ or an analogous diversity metric (does the method generate genuinely diverse candidates, or does it amplify a single mode?); (3)~CCR (does the method preferentially correct errors, or does it preferentially confirm them?). A method with $\\text{BR} < 1$, high $K_\\text{eff}$, and $\\text{CCR} > 0$ demonstrates genuine self-improvement; $\\text{BR} > 1$ with $\\text{CCR} < 0$ indicates that the method suffers from SCB regardless of aggregate accuracy gains, which may reflect favorable base rates rather than corrective signal.",
          "+",
          " \\paragraph{Extended limitations.}",
          " ",
          " Beyond the limitations discussed in the main text, several additional constraints bound our conclusions. Seven of our eleven model-domain conditions have $n \\leq 60$, providing insufficient statistical power for confirmatory claims; power analysis shows that the minimum detectable effect exceeds 5\\,pp for these conditions (Table~\\ref{tab:power}), so they serve as regime characterization rather than definitive tests. Our extraction prompts use a single zero-shot template per domain; alternative prompting strategies (few-shot exemplars, chain-of-thought extraction, or iterative refinement) might yield higher-quality constraints, though the oracle gap suggests the bottleneck is structural rather than prompt-specific. The constraint quality analysis (91.8\\% discriminative rate, 39.2\\% source-trace minority weight) covers Qwen2.5-14B on FOLIO + ProofWriter ($N = 804$); other model-domain combinations may exhibit different characteristics. Constraint extraction uses $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$ while trace sampling uses $T = 0.7$; alternative extraction temperatures might yield different results. Although we prompt for first-order logic formalizations, 99.0\\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\\% using true quantifiers ($\\forall$/$\\exists$); whether models capable of genuine quantified reasoning would yield higher discriminative rates is untested. Deduplication uses syntactic equivalence (global average dedup ratio: 46.0\\%), so semantically equivalent constraints with different surface forms receive separate weights; Z3-based semantic deduplication removes an additional ${\\sim}$10\\% of constraints and yields a small but significant improvement over no deduplication ($p = 0.013$), suggesting room for better dedup strategies. The GSM8K pilot ($n = 30$) illustrates the constraint-absent regime and should not be interpreted as confirmatory evidence."
        ]
      }

          " ",
          " \\paragraph{SCB as an evaluation framework.}",
          " \\label{app:scb-evaluation}",
          "-The SCB metrics---BR, $K_\\text{eff}$, ProcessSim, and CCR---can serve as a general evaluation framework for self-improvement methods beyond SICA. Any method that extracts structured representations from a model's own outputs (self-verification \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, chain-of-verification \\citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification}, self-consistency \\citep{wang2023selfconsistency}) faces the distributional prior that generates SCB. We propose that future work on self-improvement methods report: (1)~BR on error cases (does the method's verification signal correlate with the model's majority answer, or does it provide independent information?); (2)~$K_\\text{eff}$ or an analogous diversity metric (does the method generate genuinely diverse candidates, or does it amplify a single mode?); (3)~CCR (does the method preferentially correct errors, or does it preferentially confirm them?). A method with $\\text{BR} < 1$, high $K_\\text{eff}$, and $\\text{CCR} > 0$ demonstrates genuine self-improvement; $\\text{BR} > 1$ with $\\text{CCR} < 0$ indicates that the method suffers from SCB regardless of aggregate accuracy gains, which may reflect favorable base rates rather than corrective signal.",
          "+The SCB metrics---BR, $K_\\text{eff}$, ProcessSim, and CCR---can serve as a general evaluation framework for self-improvement methods beyond SICA. Any method that extracts structured representations from a model's own outputs (self-verification \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}, self-refinement \\citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, chain-of-verification \\citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification}, self-consistency \\citep{wang2023sc}) faces the distributional prior that generates SCB. We propose that future work on self-improvement methods report: (1)~BR on error cases (does the method's verification signal correlate with the model's majority answer, or does it provide independent information?); (2)~$K_\\text{eff}$ or an analogous diversity metric (does the method generate genuinely diverse candidates, or does it amplify a single mode?); (3)~CCR (does the method preferentially correct errors, or does it preferentially confirm them?). A method with $\\text{BR} < 1$, high $K_\\text{eff}$, and $\\text{CCR} > 0$ demonstrates genuine self-improvement; $\\text{BR} > 1$ with $\\text{CCR} < 0$ indicates that the method suffers from SCB regardless of aggregate accuracy gains, which may reflect favorable base rates rather than corrective signal.",

          " \\paragraph{SCB diagnostic toolkit.}",
          " \\label{app:diagnostic-toolkit}",
          " We recommend four zero-cost diagnostics, computable from sampled traces alone, to predict whether symbolic self-verification will help or hurt before deployment.",
          "-\\emph{Bias Ratio} (BR; Eq.~\\ref{eq:br}): for each problem, compute the fraction of constraints supporting the majority answer, divided by majority vote share. $\\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints are biased toward the majority; $\\text{BR} \\gg 2$ (as in all nine models tested; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}) signals that extraction amplifies rather than checks the model's prior. Report as mean $\\pm$ bootstrap 95\\% CI.",
          "+\\emph{Bias Ratio} (BR; \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}): for each problem, compute the fraction of constraints supporting the majority answer, divided by majority vote share. $\\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints are biased toward the majority; $\\text{BR} \\gg 2$ (as in all nine models tested; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}) signals that extraction amplifies rather than checks the model's prior. Report as mean $\\pm$ bootstrap 95\\% CI.",
          " \\emph{Effective-K} ($K_\\text{eff}$; Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}): compute Fleiss' $\\kappa$ across $K$ traces, then $K_\\text{eff} = 1 + (K-1)(1-\\kappa)$. Threshold: $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 1.3$ never improves in our experiments---aggregation collapses to majority voting. $K_\\text{eff} > 1.8$ is a necessary (not sufficient) condition for gain.",
          " \\emph{Degenerate Consensus} ($C_0$; \\S\\ref{sec:condorcet}): the fraction of problems where all $K$ traces agree. $C_0 > 95\\%$ renders aggregation moot; $C_0 < 5\\%$ means the model is maximally uncertain and constraints tend to amplify noise.",
          " \\emph{ProcessSim} (Eq.~\\ref{eq:process-sim}): mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace vectors. $\\text{ProcessSim} > 0.6$ indicates homogeneous reasoning; combined with $\\text{BR} > 2$, this predicts SCB will dominate."

{
  "text": "[写作进展] Patch — 强化 Discussion actionable takeaways 完成\n\n## 修改清单\n\n### 主文修改（discussion.tex）\nDiscussion 从原 1 段压缩摘要扩展为 3 个结构化 \\paragraph{}：\n\n1. **Practitioner decision framework** — 三个零成本诊断指标（K_eff ≤ 1.3 → 退化为 majority voting；SC < 40% → 约束放大错误；C₀ < 5% → 聚合无意义），明确中间 regime（SC ~50-60%, K_eff > 1.8）为唯一可能获益区间\n2. **SCB beyond SICA** — 推广到 self-verification / self-refinement / chain-of-verification，debate control（κ=0.15, -18.6pp）证实独立性与质量在同模型约束下不可兼得\n3. **Design principles for escaping SCB** — 三个逃逸条件（structural independence / positive capability gap / task-verifier alignment），27 个同模型干预失败 vs 406M encoder 成功（+11.00pp），trained extractors 为最有前景方向，结尾\"symbolic mirage\"总结句兼作 Conclusion\n\n### §6 压缩（solutions.tex）\n- \"Why it works\" 压缩：移除 (i)(ii)(iii) 详细描述，改为引用 §7 design principles\n- \"Capability gap\" 压缩：移除 Spearman 细节部分文字，追加 2×2 矩阵交叉引用\n- \"Regime-dependent bottleneck\" 整段删除（~7行）\n- DRC 压缩至 3 行\n\n### Appendix 扩展（appendix.tex）\nExtended Discussion 新增 3 个 \\paragraph{}：\n\n1. **SCB diagnostic toolkit** — BR / K_eff / C₀ / ProcessSim 四个指标的计算方法、阈值建议、决策规则\n2. **Future directions: trained extractors and beyond** — trained constraint extractors / multi-model ensembles / external knowledge grounding / SCB-aware aggregation\n3. **SCB as an evaluation framework** — 提议未来 self-improvement 方法应报告 BR / K_eff / CCR，定义 genuine self-improvement vs SCB-dominated 的判据\n\n### 编译验证\n- ✅ 0 critical warnings\n- ✅ 主文 8 页（Discussion 在 page 8 右栏完整结束，Limitations 从 page 9 开始，不计入页数限制）\n- ✅ 总 26 页（含 appendix）\n- 使用 \\enlargethispage{10\\baselineskip} + \\vspace{-4mm} 适配页面空间",

}

    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

_source line 21211 · 2026-05-22T10:36:03.675Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Du8k85TvhxH3ceRcNypNMS`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-Consistency and extensions.}
5	SC selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions include universal SC for free-form generation \citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting with step-aware verification \citep{li2023diverse}, program-based vote filtering \citep{toh2024notallvotes}, and reasoning-aware filtering \citep{wan2024rasc, kim2026reasc}. \citet{loo2025reevaluating} show that SC gains plateau early; \citet{byerly2026selfconsistency} further analyze SC's limitations in mathematical reasoning. Alternative aggregation strategies beyond majority voting \citep{ai2025beyondmajority} have been proposed but do not address the underlying correlation problem. Our Condorcet analysis explains this: $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ compresses $K_{\text{eff}}$ to 1.20--3.62 for $K = 12$ (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
6	
7	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
8	We distinguish three paradigms. \emph{Specification-generation} (Logic-LM \citep{pan2023logiclm}, LINC \citep{olausson2023linc}, SatLM \citep{ye2024satlm}) translates problem descriptions into formal specifications before any answer is generated; LINC documents ${\sim}$40\% translation errors even at this level. \emph{Program-generation} \citep{gao2023pal,chen2023program,lyu2023faithful} produces executable code from problem statements. Both paradigms access the problem before generating candidate answers. \emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc on reasoning traces that already contain candidates, inheriting confirmation bias (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). SatLM provides a natural control: it succeeds because problem-level formalization precedes answer generation, whereas trace-level extraction cannot avoid contamination (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). Recent extensions \citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku} introduce capabilities beyond the reasoning model. \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization degrades with complexity; \citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Our oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\,pp ProofWriter); the failure localizes to self-extraction (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).
9	
10	\paragraph{Test-time compute and verification.}
11	Scaling test-time computation through additional traces \citep{snell2025scaling} or structured search \citep{yao2023tree} faces the same correlation challenge: same-model attempts collapse into correlated solutions. Reasoning models (o1, DeepSeek-R1 \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \citep{qwen2024qwq}) extend thinking time but remain subject to correlated same-model voting: QwQ-32B produces $\kappa = 0.91$ and $C_0 = 2.6\%$, suggesting that longer reasoning chains intensify homogenization rather than alleviating it. Trained verifiers offer an alternative \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}; multi-agent debate refines answers through discussion \citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}. Self-verification \citep{weng2023selfverification}, chain-of-verification \citep{dhuliawala2023chainofverification}, self-refinement \citep{madaan2023selfrefine}, tool-augmented critique \citep{gou2024critic}, and formal CoT verification \citep{zhu2025vericot} report gains, but none systematically tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers. We find the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of verification formalism (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Self-correction without external feedback degrades performance \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}; our diagnostics (BR, Eff-$K$, process-$\kappa$) predict when trace-aggregation will fail.
12	
13	\paragraph{CJT and positioning.}
14	CJT \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees convergence under independent voting; \citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size; \citet{lefort2024condorcet_llm} apply CJT to LLM classification. \citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs. Concurrent work by \citet{kuai2026independent} proposes BEI and CIG to audit cross-model behavioral dependencies; our process-$\kappa$ and $K_\text{eff}$ are complementary, operating at the reasoning-process level within same-model traces and identifying a specific causal mechanism (SCB) that behavioral metrics miss.
15	Prior self-correction studies \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification} establish qualitative negative results; we contribute quantitative diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_D$). SCB is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}: the latter concern whether stated reasoning reflects actual computation, while SCB concerns whether \emph{self-extracted formal constraints} mirror the model's answer distribution. Formal Z3-checked verification inherits the same bias ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$)---invisible to prior work on natural-language self-correction.
```
